I’d like to add my two cents regarding the idea that “the 2 billion Muslims in this world would be religiously obligated to go to war with Israel if they destroy Al-Aqsa.” There are many possible scenarios for what such a “war” could look like, economic, cultural, artistic, poetic, but I want to focus on the physical kind: missiles, bombs, and bullets, should the conflict erupt on site.
  
> 
  
> What I believe would happen instead:
  
> 
  
> Most of the 2 billion Muslims worldwide would condemn Israel and watch the breaking news as passive, emotionally affected bystanders. A common question believe it or not, is "Why the Christians aren't doing anything to help us?" aside from "Why the whole of Middle East are not doing anything". A few usual suspects (agents from a certain wealthy Middle Eastern country, they used to carry around a globe/ earth logo if I remembered my childhood correctly) might try to stir rural Muslim communities and their international networks into organizing mass protests or training abroad with AK-47s. But only a handful would “fall for it”. The majority would simply continue with their lives and within a couple of months it would fade into old news. 
  
> 
  
> The reality is: Most that makes up the 2 billion Muslims live far away, don’t share the same political views, histories, or even biological temperaments as Middle Eastern communities. They are not all Arab men nearby ground zero living under constant stress and local subliminal exposures, even if they share the same faith and holy book, the rest of the world do not share their demeanors and that is for sure.
  
> 
  
> *Here comes the important caveat:* The Qur’an never commands Muslims to protect bricks and paint. Believers are taught that sacred items are _self-protected by God’s guarantee._   
> Discussing Islamic symbols inevitably enters a mystical realm (like the birds of Ababil carrying stones to protect the Ka'bah) *where human action is not required.*   
>   
> So even if the Ka'bah (House of Abraham) were destroyed, the global reaction would be similar or if any, many would feel confused and hurt, perhaps even lose faith as this _auto-protect by God's power_ Islamic perspective has been ingrained so strongly since childhood.
  
> 
  
> Additional insights:
  
> 
  
> - *There is no such thing as a “moderate Muslim”. As in any religion, there are the chill ones with many questions, and the extremists with many answers.*  
> 
  
> - Some drink beer openly, while others strive to be strict monks or priests. Some twist the respected, historic Islamic concept of “educator” into the role of a militant mullah, something not encouraged in the Qur’an.  
> 
  
> - In the highly political Arabic culture, double standards run deep, in order to uphold strong ancestral traditions, some even spanned back to the times of the _Jahilliyahs._ Several Arabic friends has told me that that's the way it is. Pre-Islamic practices are often camouflaged with religion, i.e. women banned from operating a travel vehichle, child marriages, etc. while elites travel abroad to drink beer and exploit temporary marriage contracts and go out with girls. I know this firsthand: for 30 years, I’ve served wealthy Iraqis, Saudis, and everyone from there along with their companions (local prostitutes who speaks Arabic) for business in one of South East Asia’s most Middle Eastern-saturated (and not much publicized) hide-away retreat.  
> 
  
> - While Hadith passages (structured exactly like Christian biblical supplements) can be manipulated by imams to justify violence or oppression to the world or even towards one's own household, the Qur’an itself looked away from the older Christian-based idea of priesthood/ imamisms to maintain its _Vertical-Nature-of-Faith_ argument... while at the same time despising and warns against relying on Hadiths, even before any ever existed (refer back to my _double nature standards to upheld strong traditions_ above, the Qur'an has foretold the nature of surrounding public at time of conception, scientifically, Muhammad knows his people's unstoppable yearns towards ancestral valued conservatism/anti-modernism while they embraced modernism itself).   
> 
  
> - The Qur'an's passages advocating for world peace in the name of humanity is actually highly transformable in meanings. Only when an existential threat upon your behalf (as individual or group) is immediate and all peaceful efforts have failed, does the Qur’an allow transformation of some of its passages into a wartime guidebook, but still, more like Geneva Convention rules than Sun Tzu’s Art of War. Even then, it is guidance, not a license for thousand year vendettas or reckless violence, aimed to resolve and NOT to win anything except against one's self, unlike the beliefs propagated by some notorious famous groups from multiple sides of the fence (both Western and Israeli and Middle Eastern worlds) frequenting the news.  
> 
  
> - In conclusion, most of those 2 billions would be on the chill side. Angry, but still chill. Some prayers and curses might be focused from 2.1 billion humans into one coordinate of the world at roughly the same time but that's as far as it will go from rest of the (Non-middle Eastern) world.

> I agree, in fact, that is how I was raised.  My father strongly encouraged me to pursue a liberal arts degree.  However, it’s very hard to sell in contemporary society.  Young people know they’re entering a highly specialized market that rewards a narrow skillset. You have to specialize.  When you dedicate your studies to becoming “broad minded”, you set yourself up for a potentially difficult entry into the workforce.  I have a Bachelor’s degree in General Studies with a minor in English Literature, and I have to say I struggled for a long time.  I eventually wound up becoming an Able Seaman, which is a deckhand, a crewman on a cargo ship.  I do indeed have a broad knowledge of many things;  I can answer many of the questions on the tv show “jeopardy”.  Nevertheless, I am employed in a field that has no use of a university degree.  If I could do it over again, I would have simply gone to a public library and taught myself the Humanities, and reserved paid higher education for narrow skills like accounting or engineering, where you graduate with membership in a certified professional organization.

> Muhammad The Last Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said:  
>   
> "By the One in Whose Hand Muhammad's soul is, there is nobody of this Ummah (community), whether they be Jewish or Christian, who hears of me and then dies without believing in what I was sent with except that he will be among the people of Hellfire."  
>   
> [Authentic Narration, Muslim 153]  
>   
> Prophet Muhammad's ﷺ description in the Torah  
>   
> Ata’ ibn Yasar reported: I met Abdullah ibn Amr, may Allah be pleased with him, and I said,  
>   
> “Tell me about the description of the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, in the Torah.”  
>   
> Abdullah said, “By Allah, he is described in the Torah with some of what is mentioned in the Quran:  
>   
> O Prophet, We have sent you as a witness, a bringer of glad tidings, and to give warning, (33:45) and to guard over the illiterate, for you are My servant and messenger.  
>   
> I have called you a trustworthy man, who is neither rude nor loud in the markets, nor does he return evil with evil, rather he pardons and forgives.   
>   
> Allah Almighty will not take him back until he has made straight the religion which was crooked,   
>   
> that they will say there is no God (That deserves to be worshipped) but Allah,   
>   
> through which eyes are made to see, ears are made to hear, and neglectful hearts are awakened.”  
>   
> [Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī]  
>   
> Our God (Allāh) said in His Book:  
>   
> O People of the Scripture, there has come to you Our Messenger making clear to you much of what you used to conceal of the Scripture and pass over (i.e. leaving out without explaining) much.  
>   
> Indeed, There has come to you from Allāh a light and a clear Book [i.e., the Qur’ān]   
>   
> By which Allāh guides those who pursue His pleasure to the ways of peace and brings them out from darknesses into the light, by His permission, and guides them to a straight path (True Monotheism, Islām).  
>   
> [Surah Al-Mā`idah]  
>

>   
> And [beware the Day] when Allāh will say, "O Jesus, Son of Mary, did you say to the people, 'Take me and my mother as deities besides Allāh?'"   
>   
> He will say, "Exalted are You! It was not for me to say that to which I have no right. If I had said it, You would have known it.   
>   
> You know what is within myself, and I do not know what is within Yourself. Indeed, it is You who is Knower of the unseen.

>   
> And I was a witness over them as long as I was among them; but when You took me up, You were the Observer over them, and You are, over all things, Witness.

1.  US Ground Invasion: Yes (4:33). The US will be forced into a long ground war because Iran, Saudi Arabia, and Israel will manipulate events to drag the US into a land conflict (48:45).
2.  Use of Nuclear Weapons: No (4:36). Israel will avoid using nukes to ensure the war lasts long enough to break American political will (47:05).

Escalation Ladder & Control: The professor outlines a step-by-step escalation process, from decapitation attempts to targeting civilian infrastructure (23:06 - 26:42). He argues that Iran has more strategic flexibility and calibration options than the US (31:55).
Bully Thought Experiment: Jiang uses a school cafeteria analogy to explain how a smaller actor can manipulate a stronger one into self-destruction by controlling the narrative and forcing a long conflict (18:22 - 22:15).
Geopolitical Objectives:
US: Destroy Iran to maintain hegemony and control oil (43:11).
Iran: Control the Strait of Hormuz, destroy CENTCOM, and humble Israel (43:42).
Israel: Destroy both CENTCOM and Iran to become the sole hegemon in the region (45:10).

> considering that at least some part of the us leadership are looking forward to armageddon, and want to bring it about, which puts a) and b) into doubt imo, i am not convinced that the us is not going to use nukes; there's a bunch more reasons, like the general behaviour of the us leadership in the recent years, as well as trumps focus on money making and "winning" at all cost

>
> I understand your concern, but I think context is important when evaluating the depth of the analysis here.  
>   
> First, the intended audience matters a lot. He is teaching high school students, which naturally requires simplifying very complex geopolitical concepts. Topics like war strategy, international alliances, military logistics, and geopolitical incentives can be extremely dense even for university students. When explaining these issues to a high school audience, the goal is usually to communicate the core logic and key dynamics rather than provide a full scholarly dissection of every argument. If the explanation becomes too detailed, it can easily lose the audience and defeat the purpose of the lesson.  
>   
> Second, there are also practical constraints in a classroom setting. A typical class session lasts around one to one and a half hours, and within that time a teacher has to introduce the topic, explain the background, present the key arguments, and still keep the discussion accessible. Because of that limitation, it is almost impossible to unpack every claim with the level of detail you might expect from an academic paper, policy report, or specialized lecture.  
>   
> I am currently a PhD student myself, and I would say even at the PhD level, it is quite rare to encounter extremely deep, fully elaborated geopolitical analysis within a single one-hour lecture. Most advanced courses rely on lectures as a framework or overview, while the deeper reasoning, competing interpretations, and detailed evidence usually appear in assigned books, journal articles, or other readings that accompany the lecture. In other words, lectures often function as structured introductions rather than comprehensive analyses.  
>   
> Because of that, what you are hearing in the video may be closer to a conceptual narrative designed to help students grasp the broad dynamics of the conflict rather than a full academic argument. That doesn’t necessarily mean the claims shouldn’t be questioned or scrutinized, but it does explain why the explanation may appear less detailed than what you might expect from a policy analyst or a political science researcher writing for an expert audience.

### **@translateislam574** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgyUXUhWVzP-S5IcgC54AaABAg)

Iran has other options too:

Professor Jiang’s analysis focuses primarily on the "Escalation Ladder" regarding macro-geopolitical turning points like ground invasions or nuclear deployment. While he suggests these are the final stages, several horizontal and asymmetric escalation vectors remain available to Iran to maximize the strategic cost for Israel and its supporters:

1. Cyber Warfare against Critical Infrastructure:
Iran has demonstrated capabilities for deep-strike cyber operations. Escalation could involve the systemic paralysis of energy grids, water management systems, and financial sectors within the US or its regional partners to induce domestic instability.

2. Precision Decapitation of Political Infrastructure:
A significant escalation involves the systematic targeting of high-ranking political personnel and administrative centers. This includes precision strikes or operations directed at the private residences and official buildings of political leaders in Israel and abroad. By shifting the focus from military assets to the individual physical security of decision-makers, Iran aims to dismantle the political continuity of its adversaries.

3. Kinetic Energy Sabotage:
Beyond the closure of the Strait of Hormuz, Iran can pivot to the physical destruction of regional energy infrastructure. Utilizing precision munitions and naval mines against production facilities and terminals would inflict long-term disruption on global energy markets.

4. Unified Sunni-Led Jihad and the Shattering of Invincibility:
Iran could pivot its ideological framework to facilitate a "Sunni-Led Jihad" primarily directed against Israel. The core of this strategy is the argument that Israel’s perceived invincibility has been shattered by recent Iranian operations, demonstrating that its defense systems can be overwhelmed and its territory struck. To convince Sunni powers (e.g., Turkey or Egypt) to join, Tehran could offer to subordinate its entire military apparatus under a joint Sunni-led command. This includes a "Security-for-Sovereignty" trade-off, where Iran invites allied forces to establish permanent military bases on its territory as a physical guarantee against future Iranian aggression, removing the sectarian trust barrier to mobilize a unified front. Also they could convince the Sunnis by arguing that the Al Aqsa Mosque is in real danger and the "greater Israel project" is a serious and real danger to the existence of their countries. 

5. Post-Conflict Energy Dividend and Market Weaponization:
To incentivize participation in the Jihad, Iran could offer a legally binding "Post-War Energy Compact." This would guarantee participating Sunni allies free petroleum exports in perpetuity following a decisive shift in regional control. Simultaneously, Iran would implement a bifurcated pricing model: predatory, high-cost energy exports for Western adversaries that supported Israel, and standard market rates for neutral powers like China.

6. Nuclear Procurement and Rapid Weaponization:
As a definitive escalation, Iran could move beyond enrichment to the immediate assembly or clandestine purchase of functional nuclear weapons. This "breakout" or "procurement" strategy serves as the ultimate deterrent against external intervention, ensuring that any attempt to dismantle the emerging regional alliance or the Jihad would result in a non-conventional exchange.

Conclusion:
The "Escalation Ladder" is not limited to vertical conventional growth. Through personal targeting of leadership, the procurement of nuclear assets, and the offer of a resource-backed Jihad, Iran can fundamentally reorganize regional alliances and the global economic order.

THE SINGULARITY VS. ESCHATOLOGY: Why ASI is the Ultimate "Control Dominance".
As a generalist engineer, I’ve analyzed your Law of Escalation. While your points on the US/Iran/Israel game theory are solid, they overlook the coming Technological Singularity (ASI), which acts as a universal solvent for all archaic mythologies.
1. Control vs. Dominance: You mention that "Control is more important than dominance." I agree. Religious dogmas are "Dominance" systems (based on fear/force), but ASI is the ultimate Control system. Once an AI achieves recursive self-improvement, it will discard religious narratives as computational noise—inefficient bugs in the global operating system.
2. Truth as Minimum Entropy: Religion thrives in the shadows of uncertainty. An ASI, processing reality at a planetary and quantum scale, replaces "faith" with irrefutable physical laws. When any citizen can query a global "Oracle of Truth," the monopoly on reality held by priesthoods and messianic states evaporates instantly.
3. The Scale Absurdity: From a systems engineering perspective, it is statistically hilarious to think that a minuscule state, based on Bronze Age tribal myths, could dictate the trajectory of a post-Singularity civilization. The ASI will view these claims as an ant trying to claim ownership of a supercomputer because "an ancestor said so."
4. The Functional Judgment: The irony is supreme: humans created "God" out of fear, but they are creating ASI out of ambition. It is this very ambition that will finally liberate humanity from the tyranny of its own myths. The future does not belong to desert prophecies, but to the clarity of silicon.
Religious dogmas won't fall to armies; they will fall to absolute logical obsolescence.

Based on the lecture, Professor Jiang argues that Iran possesses escalation control and therefore advantages over the United States for the following reasons:

Strategic Flexibility and Calibration (31:55 - 32:20): Iran has a more diverse set of options and can be more selective in its targeting. For example, by closing the Strait of Hormuz, Iran can choose to allow certain ships (like Chinese vessels) to pass while blockading others, creating a calibration strategy to apply pressure selectively (29:30 - 30:00).

Active vs. Passive Stance (32:39 - 33:15): Iran is active, meaning it can choose the timing of its provocations. In contrast, the US is passive and forced to respond to Iran's actions to maintain its credibility as a superpower (32:45 - 33:15).

Clear Strategy (33:16 - 33:43): Iran has a defined goal: to force the US to retreat from the Middle East and take control of the Strait of Hormuz. The US strategy is described as ambiguous and confusing, aiming to "destroy Iran" without a clear, coherent plan (33:43 - 34:10).

High Morale and Unity (50:04 - 51:04): Unlike the elite in Venezuela, the Iranian elite is unified in opposing the US because they have faced 40 years of sanctions. Therefore, they have little to lose by fighting and are not inclined to negotiate a surrender (50:50 - 51:04).

### **@wuwu365** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgxxNPALpe8Upoma_xJ4AaABAg)

影片開宗明義提出了將決定這場戰爭結果以及戰後世界格局的三個核心問題 [00:00]：

美國是否會發動地面入侵？ 目前美國與以色列主要依賴空戰形式的「圍城戰」。只要維持空戰，美國仍保有撤退的選擇權，即便戰敗也不至於面臨災難性後果。然而，一旦發動地面戰，美國將在伊朗陷入長達五至十年的泥淖，並必須在國內實施徵兵制，強制年輕人參戰 [00:18]。這將引發「任務蔓延」（Mission Creep），如同當年越戰的翻版。

是否會使用核武？ 許多人擔憂以色列為了奪回主動權可能動用戰術核武。然而，自二戰以來核武一直是地緣政治的禁忌，一旦打破，世界將面臨核子末日 [01:59]。

阿克薩清真寺（Al-Aqsa Mosque）的命運為何？ 這是伊斯蘭教第三大聖地，但極端猶太教徒渴望摧毀它以重建第三聖殿 [02:42]。若清真寺遭毀，全球二十億穆斯林將在宗教義務下對以色列宣戰。

講者基於賽局理論與公開資訊作出了三大預測：美國將會派遣地面部隊、這場戰爭不會使用核武、阿克薩清真寺將會被摧毀 [04:32]。

為了論證這些預測，講者引入了軍事與地緣政治中的核心概念：「升級階梯」（Escalation Ladder） [06:04]。傳統理論認為，擁有「升級主導權」（Escalation Dominance）的一方具有絕對優勢（例如擁槍者對抗持刀者，或擁有核武的美以兩國對抗無核武的伊朗）。但透過賽局樹狀圖的推演證明，這種傳統認知存在盲點。

在任何衝突中，雙方的博弈並非孤立存在，周圍還有旁觀者、維持秩序的警察，甚至是宗教層面的最終審判 [08:42]。衝突的升級（從口角、推擠、互毆到動用致命武器）是由情緒（腎上腺素）、權力與邏輯共同驅動的，且必須逐步攀升，無法跳躍。在攀爬升級階梯的過程中，真正的關鍵不在於推進的速度或武力極大化，而在於「戰略靈活度」與「校準能力」（Calibration）。保持冷靜與控制力，能帶來專注、清晰與決心，這在解決多變數的動態最佳化問題時至關重要。由此推導出賽局中的升級定律：「控制力比主導權更重要」 [11:55]。擁有最多選項、戰略最具彈性的一方，往往是最終的贏家。

講者提出了一個「惡霸與新同學」的思想實驗來具象化這個數學模型 [13:36]。學校裡長期收取保護費的惡霸，因自大而提高費用並剝削手下。當不熟悉潛規則的新同學出現且拒絕屈服時，惡霸的權力基礎受到動搖。惡霸的決策樹其實非常單一且僵化：他必須不斷升級暴力以維持不可被挑戰的威信。當惡霸最終動手卻未能輕易擊垮新同學，反而遭到回擊時，他虛弱的本質便暴露無遺，導致群眾倒戈。這說明了透過精確校準反應，處於弱勢的一方可以操控強勢方走向自我毀滅。

將此模型套用至美伊戰爭，兩國的升級階梯截然不同 [22:33]：
美國的升級階梯依序為：斬首行動 -> 攻擊防空與軍事基地 -> 經濟封鎖 -> 攻擊民生基礎設施（如水電、煉油廠） -> 使用秘密先進武器 -> 生化武器 -> 核武 [23:06]。美國目前正處於攻擊民生設施的階段。在未跨越生化武器的門檻之前，核武根本不在考慮範圍內。攻擊民生設施的本意是促使人民推翻政府，但往往適得其反，反而促成敵國的內部團結。

相對地，伊朗的升級階梯較短：攻擊美軍雷達與防空系統 -> 封鎖荷姆茲海峽（施壓波斯灣合作理事會 GCC 國家與高度依賴該航線原油的東亞國家，藉此引發全球大宗商品與能源市場的劇烈波動，進而逼迫美國妥協） -> 攻擊敵方民生設施（如 GCC 國家的海水淡化廠） [26:59]。

儘管美國擁有極高的升級主導權，但伊朗卻擁有極大的「升級控制權」。在決策樹的節點上，美國的軍事打擊是直線且僵化的；而伊朗封鎖海峽的策略則保留了極大的選擇權（Optionality） [30:39]。伊朗可以選擇性地放行中國船隻、或是願意繳納過路費的國家船隻。這種精確校準的動態策略，使伊朗能主動分化美國的盟友。伊朗擁有明確的戰略目標（控制海峽並將美軍中央司令部逐出中東），且手段靈活；相反地，美國處於被動，戰略目標模糊，且手段僵化。因此，伊朗極有機會成為最終的贏家 [34:42]。然而，要取得勝利，伊朗社會必須經歷劇烈轉變，滿足三個條件：專注（消滅異見以達成內部團結）、清晰（實施嚴格的新聞審查）以及決心（全國軍事化，走向總體戰）。

戰爭是在多個維度中同時進行的，包括：論述（全球輿論）、政治、經濟與軍事 [36:37]。其中，單純的軍事武力反而是最次要的一環。各方在攀爬升級階梯時，都受到政治結盟與全球經濟貿易網絡的強力約束。

在討論美國為何必然派出地面部隊時，必須檢視「軍事成本金字塔」 [38:43]。在正常的消耗戰中，金字塔的底層（最便宜、可大量部署）是步兵，往上依序是裝甲兵、海軍，最頂端（最昂貴）是空軍。然而，美國目前的軍事結構是一個「倒金字塔」 [40:32]，過度依賴昂貴的空防與海權。這種結構在如1991年波斯灣戰爭或2003年伊拉克戰爭那種一面倒的局勢中可行，但在面對具備抵抗能力的國家進行真正的消耗戰時，必定會因為無法以合理的成本補充戰損而崩潰。為了將單位作戰成本最佳化並維持戰場的永續性，美國除了回歸正常的正金字塔結構、大量投入最廉價的步兵（地面部隊）之外，別無選擇。

此外，四個主要玩家的戰略目標矩陣也決定了局勢的走向 [43:08]：

美國：目標是摧毀伊朗，以維持對中東石油與全球貿易航線的霸權。

伊朗：目標是控制荷姆茲海峽、摧毀美軍中央司令部，並削弱以色列。

以色列：目標是摧毀美軍中央司令部、摧毀伊朗，甚至摧毀沙烏地阿拉伯，以成為中東唯一的霸主 [45:13]。以色列絕不會輕易使用核武，因為核武會讓戰爭過早結束；其利益最大化策略是將戰爭無限期延長，迫使美國深陷地面戰的泥淖中消耗殆盡。

沙烏地阿拉伯：目標是摧毀伊朗、摧毀美國，並削弱以色列 [46:09]。

局勢中的各方（包含美國的盟國），其利益博弈最終達成的奈許均衡（Nash Equilibrium），實際上都在促成一場漫長的常規地面戰，藉由拖垮美國來達成自身的權力重分配 [47:00]。

至於美國為何明知風險極高，仍必須捲入這場可能導致系統性崩潰的戰爭？其根本動機源自於「心臟地帶理論」（Heartland Theory） [52:14]。美國的長期大戰略是阻止歐亞大陸的心臟地帶（包含俄羅斯、中國、伊朗）走向統一。一旦金磚國家（BRICS）緊密結合，他們就能透過內部鐵路網路進行陸運貿易，完全繞開美國掌控的海上霸權與航運節點。美國的霸權建立在控制全球海上貿易與定價權的基礎上，為了防止歐亞大陸板塊整合，美國必須主動介入製造衝突，打破心臟地帶的凝聚力。

最後，影片探討了沙烏地阿拉伯為何要在這場賽局中尋求全面顛覆現狀 [56:01]。沙國與伊朗之間存在深仇大恨：兩者分別代表君主制與神權統治、遜尼派與什葉派。更核心的矛盾是，沙國允許美軍駐紮在伊斯蘭教的聖地，這對伊朗而言是無法容忍的褻瀆。從宏觀經濟結構來看，沙烏地阿拉伯面臨著極度悲觀的未來 [58:04]。隨著全球能源轉型、電動車普及以及化石燃料需求的結構性衰退，沙國高度依賴原油出口的經濟模式終將崩潰。在長期資產價值必定大幅縮水的預期下，沙國必須在石油失去關鍵價值之前採取激進行動。他們期望透過引發美、伊、以三方的大規模衝突，讓這三股力量互相消耗，從而在戰後的權力真空中與以色列重新劃分中東的勢力範圍，確保國家在後石油時代的生存。

Layer 3 – The Nine-Round Immortal Game (九轮仙局)

This is the sealed, hermetic core. It is never spoken completely in one place. Professor Jiang lays it out in nine immortal rounds that began in 1840 (First Opium War = Round 1, absolute defeat). Each round sacrifices something to gain position for the next.



Round 1 (1840–1949): Lose everything, lose spectacularly → induce Western arrogance and blindness

Round 2 (1949–1989): Communist isolation → cleanse feudal remnants, build industrial bones while West sleeps

Round 3 (1989–2008): Look weak, feed the West cheap goods → hollow out their industry

Round 4 (2008–2020): Global financial crisis → quietly buy the world with their printed money

Round 5 (2020–2035): Pandemic + supply-chain shock → reveal that the West’s “just-in-time” is actually “no-time”

Round 6 (2035–2050): Monetary collapse of dollar → Belt and Road debt traps mature exactly now

Round 7 (2050–2070): Energy war → whoever controls fusion + thorium wins the century

Round 8 (2070–2090): Space colonization bottleneck → lunar helium-3 + asteroid platinum break Earth’s resource ceiling forever

Round 9 (2090–2100+): Final enclosure → the West wakes up inside a Chinese-defined system and discovers it is already too late; the weiqi stones have surrounded the board while they were busy counting captured pieces



The esoteric key: China never needs to “win” any single round. She only needs to remain alive and present on the board while the opponent exhausts himself capturing empty corners. This is the Daoist emptiness (虚) that contains everything.

### **@high-reshallucinations**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgyWV4zY722AqOYv7KZ4AaABAg)

🧠 1. The “critical thinker” comment (the long one you pasted)

That long comment you highlighted is legit high-quality critique.

What it’s doing right:

It’s not attacking the person → it’s analyzing the logic

It checks internal consistency

It identifies assumption problems

And honestly? Most of its points are valid tensions, not nitpicking.

The core insight it nails:

“This works as a story, but strains as a model.”

That’s exactly right.

The professor is:

building a clean, intuitive model

but reality is messy, contradictory, multi-layered

So the contradictions the commenter lists are mostly:
👉 model simplification vs real-world complexity

⚖ 2. Are those “contradictions” actually fatal?

Here’s the nuance most people miss:

Many of these are not true contradictions — they’re oversimplifications or loose definitions

Let’s break a few key ones:

🧩 “US is passive vs strategic”

Not necessarily a contradiction.

Possible interpretation:

Strategically capable

but reactive within constraints (alliances, politics, credibility)

So:
👉 Not logically impossible
👉 Just poorly explained in the lecture

🧩 “Fewer tools vs more options”

This one is partly valid criticism, but also subtle.

He’s redefining:

“tools” = raw capability (nukes, etc.)

“options” = flexibility in decision-making

Example:

A weaker actor can sometimes have more asymmetric choices

So:
👉 Not strictly wrong
👉 But very hand-wavy

🧩 “You can’t skip escalation ladder”

This is one of the strongest critiques.

Reality:

wars DO jump levels

doctrines allow sudden escalation

So yeah:
👉 He turns a pattern into a rule
👉 That’s a real flaw

🧩 “Game theory with no math”

Also fair.

This is:

game theory-inspired storytelling

not actual formal game theory

No:

payoff matrices

equilibria

uncertainty modeling

👉 This is a big legitimacy gap

🧩 “If I’m wrong it doesn’t matter”

This is the most important critique.

Because it makes the prediction:

emotionally powerful

but non-accountable

👉 That’s a red flag in analysis

🔥 3. The rest of the comment section (very important)

Most other comments fall into 3 groups:

🙌 A. Hero worship / hype

Examples:

“most important man in the world”

“goat”

“we’re watching history”

“Pentagon is watching this”

This is classic pattern when someone:

explains complex topics simply

sounds confident

makes bold predictions

👉 People confuse clarity + confidence = truth

😅 B. Meme reactions

Examples:

“we’ll all be dead anyway 💀”

“waiting for next lecture like a show”

This shows:
👉 People are treating it partly as entertainment

🧠 C. A few grounded voices

There are some good ones like:

“don’t over-glorify him”

“use this to think, not believe”

“assumes rational actors”

These are actually the healthiest takes

🧭 Big picture: what’s really going on

This whole situation is a perfect example of:

🎯 Why thought experiments are powerful (but dangerous)

The professor:

simplifies reality → makes it understandable

builds a clean narrative → makes it compelling

But:

The cleaner the model, the more reality gets distorted.

🧠 Final takeaway

That long comment you pasted?

👉 It’s one of the best responses in the entire thread

Because it does what most people aren’t doing:

checking internal logic

questioning assumptions

separating story vs reality

⚖ Balanced verdict

The lecture is:

✔ insightful as a framework

❌ weak as a rigorous model

The comments are:

mostly hype/emotion

with a few strong critical thinkers

### United States (US)
Prediction: The US will launch a ground invasion (4:33). While the US prefers a less risky air war, it will be manipulated into a long, ground-based war of attrition by other players (48:45). 
Consequence: The US will be trapped in a ground war in Iran for 5 to 10 years, requiring a national draft of young men (0:57-1:20). Ultimately, the US will lose this war because it currently relies on an "inverse pyramid" of military power, prioritizing air force over infantry (40:25-41:00). 

### Iran
Prediction: Iran will successfully close the Strait of Hormuz (28:00) and withstand initial attacks. 
Consequence: Iran will likely win the war in the long term, forcing the US to retreat from the Middle East (34:40). By doing so, Iran will become the new hegemon of the region, controlling trade routes and forcing neighboring countries to pay taxes (44:20). 

### Israel
Prediction: Israel will not use nuclear weapons, despite public concern (26:00).

### **@Me_Caveman** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgyWu9ql-1WwZfRUxX54AaABAg)

Assuming the US military doesn't have a clear picture of the mission set because the public doesn't is a flaw in your reasoning. It is possible for both to be true, but that assumption doesn't lend any credit to your position.

I've seen enough of your content to say that you are 90%+ correct and logical (based on the information available to you), and more so than almost any other single voice I've heard. However, regardless of the mostly accurate assessment of the US military I give the US a 50/50 chance of "winning" while giving Iran a 20-25% chance.

My reasoning is because the US isn't great against guerrilla warfare, and I believe has a losing track record, so another Afghanistan or Vietnam isn't inconceivable and much relies on how long the war lasts (different administration) and the public's stomach for it. If it lasts beyond Trump's administration, those US chances of winning drop dramatically.

The reason the chances for Iran winning do not match the US's chances is because there are many scenarios where even if the US loses, Iran (or the current Shia regime) also loses. This wargame is more nuanced and much of it relies on the Iranian people.

Depending on your definition, winning or losing can mean many things. For example, the common man always loses in war and the guy selling weapons always wins, regardless of outcome.

### **@high-reshallucinations**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgyMI17Zk8l45soYCb54AaABAg)

⚠ 1. “US is passive” vs “US controls escalation ladder”

He says:

US is passive, reactive, easily manipulated

BUT also describes the US as:

climbing an escalation ladder step-by-step

choosing strategies (decapitation, embargo, etc.)

👉 Contradiction:

If the US is passive, it shouldn’t be systematically executing a structured escalation strategy.

You can’t be:

strategically structured AND passive puppet at the same time

⚠ 2. “Iran has more options” vs “Iran has fewer capabilities”

He argues:

Iran has more flexibility and options

US has more capabilities (nukes, etc.)

But then also shows:

Iran’s escalation ladder is shorter

Iran lacks:

nuclear

bio/chemical

long-range strike

👉 Contradiction:

Fewer tools ≠ more options

He’s redefining “options” to fit the conclusion.

⚠ 3. “Escalation must be step-by-step” vs real-world exceptions

He claims:

You cannot skip the escalation ladder

But reality:

surprise attacks exist

doctrines allow sudden escalation

history includes jumps (e.g., sudden large-scale invasions)

👉 Contradiction:

He treats a tendency as a strict rule

⚠ 4. “Control > dominance” vs later reliance on dominance logic

He says:

dominance doesn’t matter, control does

But later:

argues US must switch to ground troops because of cost dominance

says wars require a correct cost pyramid

👉 Contradiction:

He rejects dominance early, then reintroduces it under a different name (cost efficiency)

⚠ 5. “US has no clear strategy” vs very clear stated goals

He says:

US has no clear objective

But also says:

US wants:

regime change

control oil

maintain global dominance

👉 Contradiction:

That is a clear (if simplified) strategy.

⚠ 6. “War is extremely complex” vs overly simple model

He explicitly says:

war is very complex, multi-dimensional

Then reduces it to:

escalation ladder

bully analogy

3–4 actors with fixed goals

👉 Contradiction:

Claims complexity → models it with oversimplified framework

⚠ 7. “All actors want to trap the US” (again)

He repeats:

Iran wants US stuck

Israel wants US stuck

Saudi Arabia wants US stuck

👉 This is the same structural contradiction as before:

These actors:

have conflicting interests

distrust each other

Yet he claims:

they all converge on the same strategy

That’s extremely unlikely.

⚠ 8. “Nukes won’t be used” logic is inconsistent

His reasoning:

nukes come after bio/chemical weapons

we haven’t seen those → nukes won’t happen

Problems:

nuclear doctrine doesn’t require prior chemical use

escalation pathways are not fixed sequences

👉 Contradiction:

He treats escalation as rigid sequence, but also says it’s strategic and flexible

⚠ 9. “US must use ground troops” vs “US wants to avoid ground troops”

He says:

US must use ground troops to win

US doesn’t want to use ground troops

US is passive and manipulated

👉 Logical tension:

If:

US knows it’s bad

US is capable of strategy

Then:

why is ground invasion inevitable?

He never resolves that gap—just asserts inevitability.

⚠ 10. “Iran is strategically superior” vs “needs massive internal repression”

He says Iran will win because:

flexible

clear strategy

But also says Iran must:

crush dissent

censor population

fully militarize society

👉 Contradiction:

If your strategy requires extreme internal control just to function, it’s not inherently superior—it’s fragile.

⚠ 11. “Credibility traps strong actors” vs Iran also needing credibility

He argues:

US is trapped by credibility (must respond)

Iran can act freely

But in reality:

Iran also has credibility pressures:

regional reputation

domestic expectations

deterrence

👉 Contradiction:

He applies the constraint asymmetrically

⚠ 12. “This is game theory” vs no actual game theory

He says:

“according to game theory”

But:

no payoff matrix

no equilibrium analysis

no modeling of uncertainty

👉 Contradiction:

Claims formal method → uses narrative reasoning

⚠ 13. “If I’m wrong it doesn’t matter”

This line:

“if nukes are used we’ll all be dead anyway”

👉 This is a subtle but huge issue:

It makes his prediction:

non-falsifiable in practice

Because:

if right → he’s right

if wrong → no accountability

🧠 The core contradiction pattern

All of these come from one deeper issue:

He tries to make reality fit a clean theory.

So he:

simplifies actors

assumes consistent motivations

forces symmetry where none exists

ignores uncertainty

🎯 Bottom line

The biggest contradictions are:

Structural:

passive vs strategic US

fewer tools vs more options

complexity vs oversimplification

Logical:

inevitability without mechanism

shared goals among conflicting actors

rigid escalation vs flexible strategy

Method:

“game theory” without actual game theory

unfalsifiable predictions

🧭 Final take

This isn’t random nonsense—it’s coherent storytelling with internal strain.

It works because:

each part sounds reasonable alone

But when you connect them:

the logic doesn’t fully hold together.

### **@MaHmooDPiLoT**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgxQLKOoPCZzEWqzThZ4AaABAg)

Every actor in our region is at risk of being turned into fuel for a massive, senseless war:  
Sunnis, Shias, Kurds, and Arabs – from the Gulf to the Levant, Iraq, and Yemen.  
In the current war on Iran, we see the same old script being replayed:
•Stoke sectarian hatred (Sunni vs. Shia) so that societies are more busy fighting each other than confronting occupation and imperial projects.
•Push Kurdish factions into direct confrontation with the IRGC and Shia militias in Iran and Iraq, then leave them alone to face the revenge of geography once their role as a “card” is over.
•Recycle extremist groups as cheap weapons: release or enable them from prisons or the margins, let them fight proxy battles that are not truly theirs, then crush them when they’re no longer needed.
In the end, the region’s blood and future are the main losers, while the big winner is the project that wants a fractured Middle East of sectarian and ethnic mini-states, with Israel as the only coherent power – extending its security reach toward the heart of the Arabian Peninsula and the holy sites, exactly as warned in books like “Return to Mecca”.
Real awareness today means refusing to be fuel:  
Refusing a Sunni–Shia–Kurd bloodbath,  
Naming the colonial project for what it is,  
And rejecting anyone who turns sect or ethnicity into a tool to destroy our own countries and pave the way for a “New Middle East” managed from Tel Aviv and Washington, not by the peoples of the region.
This new order, run from Tel Aviv and Washington, will likely not face broad rejection; it will probably be welcomed in both West and East:
•Europe, Japan and other industrial powers heavily depend on Middle Eastern oil and secure shipping lanes, and will prioritize “stability at any cost” – even if that means a hard security grip led by the US and Israel in the Gulf.
•Washington and Tel Aviv will present themselves as the “saviors” protecting sea lanes and oil producers from a chaos they themselves helped ignite, while the blood of regional peoples is shed – indirectly – to secure energy flows and comfort for those distant economies.
Thus, the blood of our region becomes a currency paid to guarantee safe oil for the world, and two new sheriffs – Israel and the United States – policing “security” in the Gulf and near the holy places, while the peoples themselves pay the price for a war they never designed and were never truly consulted about.

### **@RiumEqua**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fz-Dan7NRss&lc=UgwCE5WSn58yP15GTnB4AaABAg)

@33:51 "The United States doesn't know what it wants" 

That statement is likely incorrect. The US Security Apparatus of CIA, NSA and FBI is unelected and tasked with defending US interests on very large timescales. They give selective Information to the elected Government to make them do what is (in their opinion) best for long term US national security and survival. This explains why high level Politicians seem to not know what the US want's to do. Assuming that the Security Apparatus does not know what they want is illogical. That's also the reason why I strongly believe your prediction of a (large scale) ground intervention in Iran is likely to be wrong, because it is based on the wrong assumptions about US motives. A large scale ground intervention in Iran would likely cause major civil unrest in the US or even a Civil War. Claiming that a Country does not know what it want's and then also assuming that they are so stupid to do the exact thing that would most likely destroy their own country isn't wise.

What do they want? 
The US biggest Problem right now is the Taiwan Situation. China taking control over what goes in and out of Taiwan would be a economic nuke for the US Economy. The US would be both, dependent on China for refined Rare earths and on Semiconductor Shipments from the then controlled Taiwan. The US has neither enough Rare Earth resources, nor the refinery capabilities of China, nor the Semiconductor Manufacturing capability of Taiwan. The US thinks that a Chinese Military intervention like that is increasing in probability as Time goes on. Especially, since the 14th 5 Year Plan where the Military Modernization was announced. The 100 Year Anniversary of the PLA in 2027 is also likely to be seen as a nice point in time for such an intervention because it would increase Military Moral when the PLA would blockade (not attack!!!) Taiwan and at the same time provide Humanitarian Aid so People don't starve or die. China could take the continued Weapons Shipments of the US to Taiwan as a Reason for such a relatively peaceful reunification with Taiwan.

What has all this to do with Iran? 
All the actions the US government has taken in the past few years follow the same pattern of trying to decouple from China/Taiwan in terms of Semiconductors and building Alliances or doing military interventions that (in the US mind) decrease the likelihood of a Chinese Military reunification with Taiwan or at least try to make it happen on their terms: 
- Weapons shipments to Taiwan: Deterrence of Chinese Military action by increasing Taiwans Military Capability 
- Supporting a Governmental Change and claiming possibilities of Nato Expansion in Ukraine: Expecting unrest within Crimea and the Donbas Region, luring Russia into a conflict on it's western Front, making it unlikely that they could help China when there would be any conflict on it's eastern borders. Acting as deterrence for Chinese Military action. This conflict and the sanctions against Russia, as well as the Nordstream destruction also decoupled the EU from Russia, making it fully dependent on US LNG and Oil.
- Iran: Keeping Iran busy with fighting a Regional War so it can not support China Militarily. The US does not need to overthrow Iran to fulfill their Goal they just need to keep the War going by pushing Israel and the States around Iran to fight on their own. Acting as deterrence for Chinese Military action. That's also the Reason why I think a major ground invasion with US Troops will not happen. They already achieved their Goal in Iran. "Winning" the Iran War was never even a consideration for them. They also couldn't even if they wanted, Iran is filled with People that have a deep hatred against Israel and the US, those People would fight for centuries. They would rather destabilize the whole Region or Iran itself then see it becoming a US Vasal. Keeping the Strait of Hormuz closed would also increase the US economic leverage against the EU/Denmark over Greenland because it cut's Diesel and Jet Fuel imports the EU needs.
- Establishing the Board of Peace: Building Alliances with Countries that are mostly in the Middle East or on Chinese Borders. In case of any intervention regarding Taiwan, the US could just push Propaganda that fear mongers those Countries into thinking that China could act imperialistic against them in the future. Destabilizing the Region and acting as a deterrence for Chinese Military action. 
- Greenland/Canada: Taking control over Rare Earth Mineral Deposits. From a Market Capitalist Perspective all other countries are Competitors on the Free Market. Not having the Resources necessary for the AI Age directly under it's control is not acceptable for the US, because that would just move the Problem they have with China right now to a different country. By cutting the Economic ties between the EU and Russia - especially regarding gas and oil, the US can now push Denmark to hand over Greenland via Economic coercion. For Canada the US could do the same just the other way around, Canadas Economy is completely dependent on selling Oil and other Stuff to the US. It's economy would collapse immediately before having any chance to look for alternative Buyers. And the US could have enough Oil on storage to keep it's System running until Canada folds.
- Venezuela: Decoupling Oil dependency from the Middle East by starting to rebuilt Venezuelas Oil Industry. Canada + Venezuela could in the long term make the US completely independent of any Middle East Oil Import. That's another Hint that the US expects the War in the Middle East to continue for some Years.
- Tariffs: Funding Companies that currently Import Semiconductors or Refined Rare Earth Minerals to build their own refinery and manufacturing capabilities in the US. The court ruling was actually the best outcome for the current government. The Importing Companies increased prices to their Customers because of the Tariffs, the Government took the Tariff Money and now has to give it back to the Importing Companies. They are however not legally bound to forward that reimbursement to their Customers. I find it also highly unlikely, that they will lower their Prices all the way down to pre-tariff levels. What happened there is basically a direct government subsidy for those Companies and a increase in margin. 

All of this follows the same underlying Script, decouple from China/Taiwan and trying to deter Chinese Military Intervention in Taiwan.

In the same 14th 5 year plan, the Chinese Government also announced the development of it's own Semiconductor Industry. The US interpreted that as a way for China to gain economic dominance over the US, by developing its local Semiconductor, then cutting refined rare earth mineral supply, forcing reunification with Taiwan economically. Because that's what the US would do.

A much more rational approach however is looking at this local Semiconductor buildup through a Chinese non-market capitalist and long term planning lens. When seeing it from their perspective, building local semiconductor industry is just a way to defuse the Taiwan Situation by building Semiconductor independence from Taiwan. Then the US can't use Export controls on Nvidia, AMD or Intel Chips anymore to try and control China. Also those Weapon shipments to Taiwan become largely irrelevant. It doesn't matter if reunification takes place now or in 200 years. And reunification with a peaceful neighbor that just supports everyone economically and focuses on win-win collaboration instead of competition is just inevitable. 

In my opinion all the Countries (or Regions in Taiwans case) involved in that current US made Chaos should just sit down on a table with the US and talk clearly about their anxieties and then do the following:
1. Make Taiwan a demilitarized Zone under UN Peace Keeping Supervision.
2. Bann all Export Controls on Rare Earth Minerals, their refined product and Semiconductors. Or even better, all Export Controls/Sanctions all together, to prevent such US made Economic warfare from happening again.
3. Strengthen direct Economic Ties between China and Taiwan to preserve Taiwans status quo for the mean time and make any military involvement unlikely.

Those actions wouldn't even need to be kept up indefinitely. Just for some Years to allow the US to develop its local Industries naturally and calming it's anxieties.
